# BUILD TASK LIST — agent-factory (Stage 0, end-to-end, durable)

> **This file is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for live build status.** The in-session task tool
> does NOT survive a new chat; this committed file does. In any new session: *"Read
> `todo/BUILD-TASKLIST.md`, load it into your task list, then start at the first `pending` task."*
>
> **TASK-DB MIRROR RULE (owner mandate 2026-08-10, ported from trading-system).** The container
> task DB rolls back with disk resets and reuses its slot numbers, so it silently loses completed
> programs. Standing protocol: (1) every task CREATE and every status CLOSE is mirrored into
> this file's §LIVE ledger in the SAME increment, committed and pushed; (2) on every resume the
> task DB is restored FROM this ledger + the transcript's TaskCreate/TaskUpdate record — never
> from memory; (3) task KEYS are the SUBJECT SLUGS below, never bare #N (slot numbers collide
> across containers).
>
> **Branch:** `claude/soundbox-kit-migration-iz1jwf` (designated). **Authoritative design:**
> `seeds/seed-stage0-v1.yaml` (the contract) + `tasks/stage0-breakdown.md` (the decomposition)
> + `docs/research/COUNCIL-VERDICT-STAGE0-v1.md` (why) + `docs/07_BUILD_PLAN.md` (the stage plan).
> Each row is a distillation; the SEED is the full spec.
>
> **How to execute:** one increment at a time respecting `blocked-by`; one increment = code +
> deterministic test + commit (`S0-#n: <proof> — <what>`); the main loop re-runs every gate
> before marking done. Heavy/live/container work runs ON THE PC via `scripts/pc.sh`
> (`PC-BRIDGE.md`); `NOT run here` is stated, never skipped silently.

**DECISION D-034 — MAIN-BUILD-TO-GOOD-STATE; SUBTLETIES → GITHUB ISSUES (owner directive 2026-09-17; canonical id D-034 — renumbered 2026-09-17 from a colliding "D-033", which is the older Buzz-teammate ADR, see docs/08_DECISION_LOG.md):** the main build/verify loop drives each proof to a GOOD STATE — the core capability actually proven through the real pipeline with the deterministic gates green — and STOPS there. An adversarial-verifier finding triggers a fix ROUND only if it is CORE-BLOCKING: it shows the proof's HEADLINE capability is actually fake (a real hollow-green in the core claim — e.g. O3's credential screen genuinely failing open, S0-06's bindings table authorizing empty). Every OTHER finding — oracle-completeness gaps, test-strength on a pinned artifact, edge cases, bells-and-whistles — is RAISED AS A GITHUB ISSUE (`verify-followup` + `stage0` labels; repo Pxls21/agent-factory) for later batch triage (fix in a sweep, or consciously close/"eat"), NOT looped. Rationale: the S0-02 closure-oracle treadmill (5 rounds, the core authz checker solid throughout) was diminishing returns slowing the build. The anti-hollow-green mandate still governs the CORE claim (no fake headline capability ships); it no longer forces a round for every meta-oracle subtlety. Exemplar issue: #3 (S0-02 F3 test-strength). NOT changed: the deterministic gates, the two-independent-gates rule, the EXPIRED→mint owner gate. IN FLIGHT is unaffected — O4 fixes a CORE credential fail-open (keep); B5 is the owner's sanctioned "see the next one" last oracle round for S0-02 — RESOLVED 2026-09-17: B5 LANDED 3a6c961 as good-state; the parser loop STOPPED WITHOUT a 6th-round VERIFY-B5 (B5 is test-tightening with no hollow-green risk, and its own report names the residual it would find); the durable execute-and-diff-STATE rewrite is filed as issue #5; AF-AP-96 registered. The main-CI-green piece (the ordinary suite, task #64) is part of "good state" and is tracked separately.

**Proof review status — authoritative, VISIBLE, machine-checked by `scripts/check-proof-status.py` (AF-AP-32).**
The guard binds this single visible line to the ONE canonical task row (exact proof→slug map) so
the two surfaces cannot diverge. It checks CONSISTENCY, not authenticity: while the agent pushes
under the owner's own GitHub identity, no in-repo status is structurally owner-only, so `ACCEPTED`
records an explicit OWNER PROCESS DECISION — never a machine-enforced guarantee. Making acceptance
owner-verifiable needs identity separation (a dedicated bot identity + protected `main` + the
owner's native GitHub review on the head SHA); that is the separate AF-AP-32 governance task
(`acceptance-anchor-af-ap-32`), owner-blocked on infrastructure.

**2026-09-17 00:35Z GOV2c — the independent verify caught 2 real blockers in GOV2b; both closed (27a3465); #67 GOV2 COMPLETE:**

The "never self-accept spine" rule earned its keep. The independent adversarial verify of GOV2b (a sandbox adversarial-verifier attacking the review binding through the real gpg path) returned NOT-READY with two BLOCKERS my own tests+mutation had missed — I had prematurely called GOV2b done. Reproduced both on HEAD, then closed them in GOV2c (`src/agent_factory/governance/review.py` + tests + `reviews/README.md`):
- **F1 (TOCTOU).** `verify_review` gpg-verified one read of the record path and `json.loads`-parsed a SECOND, independent read — an attacker who can write the store flips the file between them and an UNSIGNED record grants the reviewed bit (measured **117 of 400** accepted on HEAD, breaking the very "first-party store is not enough to forge" claim). Fix: read record+sig ONCE (O_NOFOLLOW), copy into the isolated keyring, verify+parse the SAME snapshot bytes → **0 of 400**.
- **F2 (GOODSIG substring + revocation).** `"GOODSIG" not in stdout` is satisfied by an attacker's `NOTATION_DATA` line, and gpg exits 0 on a revoked/expired key (`REVKEYSIG`, no `GOODSIG`) — a revoked key + a "GOODSIG" notation was ACCEPTED, silently disabling revocation. Fix: require a real `[GNUPG:] GOODSIG ` status LINE whose `VALIDSIG` fingerprint is the committed owner's → `REVKEYSIG` now **REFUSED**.
- Folded same-file follow-ups: F6 (exactly one key in the owner file, else `fubuki-owner-key-ambiguous`), F5 (absolute gpg + `--no-options` + minimal env — ambient `gpg.conf`/PATH-shadowed gpg is not a trust channel; owner key imported from the snapshot via stdin), F7 (`GovernanceError` on every error path), F8 (O_NOFOLLOW record/sig), F4 (README `PYTHONPATH=src` + a verify step).
- Gates: 6 new tests (red-first on the pre-fix code, green after); the review suite 16 passed; the full governance suite (9 files) `65 passed` ×2; pyflakes clean; the verifier's own repro script now prints `accepted: 0 of 400` and `REFUSED`; mutation 4/4 (GOODSIG-line, one-key, re-read/TOCTOU, O_NOFOLLOW each red their test), review.py restored byte-identical. Verifier HELD everything else (no self-assertion residue, content binding, path guard, reviewed-exact, absence, keyring isolation vs an unprivileged caller); its F9 (the governance suite is order-sensitive — GOV2a's `_foreign_preimports` guard vs the pin_sync test; reproduced at the parent, so pre-existing, alphabetical CI collection is green) and F12 (load_packet has no live production caller yet — GOV1 is a proposal) are INFO. #67 GOV2 COMPLETE (all of F1+F2 built+verified); GOV1 stays a PROPOSAL; nothing minted. NEXT (unchanged): #64 CI baseline; #69 ancestry gate; the PC follow-ups for #65/#68 (bridge up).

**2026-09-16 23:06Z #66 DONE — fubuki-pin-sync portable, no /dev/fd (review 2026-09-15; commit a956d3c); GOV2b trust model is with the owner:**

A decision-free review fix while GOV2b's trust model waits on the owner (below). `scripts/fubuki_pin_sync.sh` promised in its header it runs on "every venue" but filtered git's benign "shallow" note through bash process substitution (`2> >(grep …)`), a /dev/fd dependency — so on a venue without /dev/fd the clone dies and all four provisioning tests fail (the header's claim was broader than the impl). Fix: a `git_clone_quiet` helper using a tempfile in the already-created `$DEST` (no /dev/fd, no new dep like mktemp), preserving git's exit code and its non-'shallow' stderr. Tests: the four provisioning tests are the behavioral control (still pass); +2 — a source guard (no `>(`/`<(` in the script) paired with a clone-failure behavioral control (rc + real stderr preserved, tempfile cleaned) per AF-AP-80. Gates: `6 passed` ×2 identical (venv, PYTHONPATH unset); `bash -n` clean; mutation — re-add process substitution → source guard red, force rc=0 → nonrepo test red, script restored byte-identical. detect-changes: no risk. The three real venues (sandbox, PC-Fedora, CI-ubuntu) all mount /dev/fd, so this is a correctness-of-claim fix, not an active breakage. Task #66 closed.

DECISION FOR THE OWNER — GOV2b (the review-binding half of #67): VERIFY-GOV1 F2 wants the reviewed bit sourced from a first-party record keyed by the governance hash (GOV2a closed F1; today `load_packet` fails closed on every real packet because the pinned upstream emits no review field, and the old `_reviewed` read the packet's own self-asserted flag). GOV1 already proved the governance hash is deterministic for fixed sources, so a hash-keyed review record is viable. The open fork is the TRUST MODEL / vehicle for a review record, and it materially changes the build + adds a different owner step: (a) an unsigned first-party committed review store keyed by the hash (simplest; closes the packet-self-assertion channel and binds review to exact content; trust = the repo/review process); (b) a GPG-signed review record verified against the already-committed owner key (`docs/governance/owner-signing-key.asc`) — the strongest, matching the accepted-anchor pattern (#54), but the accepted-anchor verifier is git-tag-specific (`git verify-tag`), so the signed review record needs a NEW vehicle (a detached signature over the hash, or a signed tag per bootstrap hash) plus an owner signing step. Recommendation: (b) signed, since the owner chose signed for proof acceptance and this is the governance spine — but it is not mine to pick silently for a security boundary. Awaiting the owner's call; not blocking (this #66 fix landed instead).

TL;DR: llama.cpp DOES batch concurrent requests. The 02:0xZ cells were confounded twice — they ran at ~85K context, so a cold prefill dominated the end-to-end wall, and the number labelled "aggregate" was really the PER-REQUEST rate (the llama.cpp metric sums per-slot seconds). A clean re-measure (short "quick job" prompt so decode dominates, warm, each request's own `timings.predicted_per_second` + the concurrent wall) shows concurrency scales. Numbers pasted from the PC logs (`run-conctest.log`/`run-np2test.log`/`run-np2b.log`); full table in `docs/research/findings/MATRIX-L1-RESULTS.md`.

- [x] no-MTP aggregate SCALES with slots: MTP `-np 1` 62.4 t/s → no-MTP `-np 2` **70.6** (2 lanes @ 36) → no-MTP `-np 4` **93.0** (4 lanes @ 25). +45% total throughput at 4-up on ONE 3090.
- [x] MTP does NOT stack with batching: MTP `-np 2` 60.1, MTP `-np 4` 62.4 — no gain over single-stream. So concurrency ⇒ DROP MTP.
- [x] DEPLOYABLE for parallel build lanes: **no-MTP `-np 2` = 70.6 t/s**, 131K/slot (fits real lanes; `-np 4`'s 65K/slot overflows). Precondition: the Hermes-side per-lane window cap (each lane must believe its window is the slot size).
- [x] Trade: one MTP lane is fastest PER-lane (62). Batched is more TOTAL work but each lane is slower. Fire-and-forget lanes want total throughput.
- [ ] A 2nd GPU is NOT required for concurrency (it raises the ceiling; it is not the only path). vLLM is the stronger high-concurrency path (paged-attention batching + EAGLE spec-decode that STACKS with batching, removing the MTP-vs-batch trade) — re-decides the 2026-09-03 "no vLLM" call, needs a non-GGUF quant (AWQ/GPTQ/FP8); the owner's #61 decision.
- [x] INCIDENT AF-AP-91: ~7 hand restarts during the probes tripped the unit's `StartLimitBurst=3`/`5min` → server DOWN (np2b's restore logged `restart … failed 5`); recovered by `reset-failed` + `start`; healthy on the `-np 1` MTP baseline (MainPID 1068470, live).

No qwen-server default change: MTP `-np 1` stays right for ONE lane at a time; no-MTP `-np N` is the concurrency config when the owner wants parallel local lanes. The card is a bottleneck only for PER-lane peak speed, not for aggregate throughput (batching recovers ~1.45×).

**2026-09-16 02:0xZ THE L1 MATRIX is COMPLETE — the lossless levers MEASURED; the current qwen-server defaults are PROVEN optimal for ONE lane at a time (no change). ⚠ The concurrency verdict in THIS block (throughput halves / a 2nd GPU is the only way) is SUPERSEDED by the 04:44Z block above — batching HELPS; the confounded cells are kept below for the record:**

Full results + pasted cell numbers in `docs/research/findings/MATRIX-L1-RESULTS.md`. Ran live on the single 3090 at effort `medium`; every number pasted from each cell's result.json.

CONCURRENCY [⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-16 04:44Z — these cells were confounded twice (an 85K cold prefill dominated the end-to-end wall; the number labelled "aggregate" was really the PER-REQUEST rate). The clean re-measure in the 04:44Z block above shows batching HELPS: no-MTP -np 2 = 70.6 t/s, no-MTP -np 4 = 93. Kept below for the record.] (the owner's question — can -np 2 match speeds?): each cell ran two SYMMETRIC 1000-token generations at ~85K context. **A** (MTP -np 1, 1 req) 45.27 decode / 33.10 end-to-end t/s. **Aq** (MTP -np 1, 2 req) busy_slots 1.0 — they SERIALIZE, aggregate 23.45 end-to-end (WORSE than 1). **G2** (MTP -np 2, 2 concurrent) busy_slots 1.920 — both slots genuinely concurrent — aggregate 21.94 decode / 7.80 end-to-end = 0.48× A. **D2** (no-MTP -np 2) busy_slots 1.984, 16.36 decode / 7.83 end-to-end = 0.36× A. VERDICT: -np 2 engages both slots but total throughput HALVES; two lanes ≈ 0.4-0.5× one lane on one card; each lane then ~1/4 speed. The single 3090 is the SHARED bottleneck (2 slots split its compute, they don't add it); MTP's win is a -np 1 phenomenon. This VALIDATES the -np 1 default and the dispatcher's one-local-lane rule. The "89 t/s" was low-context; at ~85K single-stream is 45 t/s decode / 33 end-to-end. **A second GPU is the only way to two full-speed lanes** (the owner's pending #61 decision — answered: on one card serial beats parallel).

LOSSLESS LEVERS (all at -np 1): **Bb** (cache-ram 8192, 4-round alt) re_prefill 0; **B** (cache-ram 32768) re_prefill 0, decode 51.88 ≈ Bb 51.92 — NO gain (the default cache already holds both lane states). **Fs** (ngram-mod) decode 25.96 = 0.57× MTP — REJECT. **H** (-ub 2048) decode 46.78 (no change), no prefill gain, VRAM 23615 (~960 MiB from OOM) — REJECT. C (checkpoints) + E (q8_0) NOT run (C needs an edited-history re-prefill workload, baseline re_prefill already 0; E's q8_0 is a context trade the -np 1 262K q4_0 already covers) — deferred, not adopted. DECISION: **NO change to qwen-server.sh defaults — the current unit IS the measured optimum.** The next real speed lever is hardware or a model-server upgrade (DFlash/GDN A/B, gated, future).

TOOLING BUG FOUND + FIXED (AF-AP-89): the first dispatch DIED at pc_lane.sh's effort-check. Root cause (reproduced, not guessed): the check's `open('/proc/$(systemctl --user show -p MainPID --value qwen-builder)/cmdline')` embeds a `$(systemctl…)` inside a DOUBLE-QUOTED bridge argument, so the sandbox shell expanded it LOCALLY — the sandbox has no user bus (`systemctl --user` → empty) → the string became `/proc//cmdline` = the KERNEL cmdline → false `mismatch:unknown` → a doomed restart-queue (`qwen-builder/pending/env` absent) → die. The server was ALREADY at xhigh (verified in its live argv). FIX: pc_lane.sh now runs the MainPID lookup ON THE PC — escaped `\$(...)`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` exported, the pid passed to python as argv (never into the quoted heredoc). Validated live: `bash -n` clean, the role-table seam prints `server-effort=xhigh`, and the fixed effort read against the running server returns `match` (so the effort-queue is correctly SKIPPED). First dispatch used the `LANE_SET_SERVER_EFFORT=0` escape hatch (the server verified xhigh); the fix removes the need for it.

The AF-AP-89 fix side-steps this for the already-at-target case (EFF_STATE=match → the whole queue block is skipped), but a medium-effort local BUILD lane would still hit the missing dir — provision `qwen-builder/` (and re-check restart-when-idle's already-applied detection) before the matrix's medium cells run locally.

owner's signing key + tooling absent here), so it is SURFACED as an owner decision rather than
faked as a fourth forgeable file (#1 rule: surface the blocker). 169 full suite ×2 deterministic;

state TRANSITION is tested through EVERY adjacent gate (the cycle-6 ACCEPTED test never ran ledger

**2026-09-04 sync (S0-11 cycle 6 — the trust binding is now COMPLETE):** The owner's SIXTH review
found the cycle-5 fixes real but two safeguards still bypassable, both reproduced this session.
(1) **The attestation was an incomplete trust closure (AF-AP-31 extended).** It hashed only
`proofs/S0-11/**` — omitting the runner, validator, schemas and registry — so mutating
`scripts/proof-runner` to run every leg as `/usr/bin/true` made the run fail while the STALE green
artifact was preserved and `validate-ledger integrity` still said PRESENT; and forging the
recorded positive command to `/usr/bin/true` with a recomputed self-digest was accepted because
the validator never compared the runs to `spec.json`. Fixed three ways, each reproduced closed:
`proof_attestation` now covers the COMPLETE trust closure (runner + validator + registry + schemas
+ proof-local) so a runner/validator mutation → `attestation-mismatch`; the validator binds the
recorded runs to the attested spec one-for-one (leg/command/exit + each negative `failure_reason`)
so the forged command → `runs-spec-mismatch`; and the runner INVALIDATES (removes) the artifact on
any real non-defer failure so a mutated-runner run → `ABSENT` (only an explicit exit-2 defer
preserves). (2) **The review-status guard was a vocabulary ban, not a state check (AF-AP-32
extended).** It rejected only the literal `re-closed`, so a `DONE by coordinator` row passed.
Replaced with a structured parser (`scripts/check-proof-status.py`) that reads the canonical
`PROOF-STATUS <id> <status>` HTML-comment marker and enforces state — one marker per proof, status ∈
{REVIEW-PENDING, ACCEPTED}, ACCEPTED requires a committed `proofs/<id>/OWNER-ACCEPTED`, deletion
fails — with `tests/test_proof_status.py` covering DONE/CLOSED/deleted/duplicate/ACCEPTED cases.
167 full suite ×2 deterministic; all 4 result.json regenerated with the closure attestation +
per-negative failure_reason; ledger integrity green. Bakes: `anti-hollow-green` tactic 13 extended
(complete trust closure + runs-bound-to-spec + never preserve across a real failure); `build-loop`
review-pending rule extended (structured status parsing). Honest gap the owner named: the CHAIN
(attestation over the full closure + runs-bound-to-spec) is now checkable on ANY venue, but the
isolation RUN is verifiable only on a capable venue (the root sandbox here; the PC/gVisor over the
bridge needs a banner — none this session). S0-09/S0-10 regex checkers remain the AF-AP-25/32
siblings, still DEFERRED per the owner. **REVIEW-PENDING — awaiting the owner's acceptance.**

**2026-09-04 sync (S0-11 cycle 5 — four more; the proof is now bound to its code):** The owner's
FIFTH review found four blockers, each reproduced this session before fixing. (1) **Proof not
bound to executable inputs (AF-AP-31).** Neutering `_iso_launch` to a pass-through and re-running
`validate-ledger integrity` still returned `S0-11 PRESENT` — the validator checks schema + digest
but the digest is only over the runs, and the positive stdout hash is merely sha256("PASS\n"); CI
never re-runs the proof. Fix: the runner records an `attestation` (sha256 of every input file
under the proof dir — checker, spec, fixtures, design), the validator re-derives it from the tree
and fails on any mismatch (`attestation-mismatch: S0-11 proofs/S0-11/check_eval_hardening.py`), and
result.schema.json REQUIRES it across all four present artifacts (S0-09/10/11/12 regenerated —
their checkers untouched). (2) **Canonical runner did not defer (AF-AP-24 recurrence 3).** On an
incapable venue the real `scripts/proof-runner` removed result.json first, then failed
`leg-exit-mismatch … got 2`; and the "canonical-invocation" test invoked the checker legs
directly. Fix: the runner treats exit 2 as DEFER (preserves the artifact, exits 2); new tests
drive the REAL runner in BOTH venue states. (3) **Fresh cwd isolated but unusable (AF-AP-30
recurrence 5).** `TemporaryDirectory` is root-owned 0700, so the dropped uid could neither write it
nor (a real rubric) collect output, and it was deleted before observation returned. Fix: chown the
workspace to the drop uid, a real write-producing rubric writes it, the parent COLLECTS the output
before cleanup (workspace-not-writable fails the proof). (4) **UID drop is not a full privilege
boundary.** The root launch set neither `no_new_privs` nor cleared the capability bounding set. Fix:
`setpriv … --no-new-privs --bounding-set -all`; the parent asserts NoNewPrivs=1 and CapBnd=0. Plus
the fourth review finding — the ledger claimed the coordinator's own closure while the handoff
said review-pending (AF-AP-32): a proof the owner reopened is REVIEW-PENDING until the owner
accepts, now enforced by a `verify-planning-repo.sh` guard that bans the self-closure vocabulary. 40 S0-11 tests (attestation binding, both-venue canonical runner,
writable-cwd collection, no_new_privs mutation), 152 full suite ×2 deterministic; all 4 result.json
regenerated with attestation; ledger integrity green. Bakes: anti-hollow-green gains
artifact-to-source attestation + the real write/collect/cleanup workload rule; build-loop gains
"exercise the canonical consumer in both venue states". S0-09/S0-10 regex-checker hardening
(AF-AP-25 siblings) still DEFERRED per the owner's instruction — only their artifacts were
re-stamped, their checkers untouched. **REVIEW-PENDING — awaiting the owner's acceptance.**

**2026-09-04 sync (S0-11 round 3 — structural fixes, class not specimen):** A third owner review
showed the round-2 fix passed its own cases but the same SECURITY CLASSES bypassed one equivalent
out. The meta-lesson (AF-AP-30): fixing named specimens is not closing a class. Five structural
fixes: (1) evidence is now PARENT-OBSERVED — the child only signals ready and blocks; the parent
reads kernel truth from `/proc/<pid>/status` (real uid), `/ns/net` (netns), `/environ` (env), and
`nsenter`s the child netns to prove a held listener is unreachable. Child self-reports are never
trusted (a fake wrapper fabricates a clean one; `unshare --user --net` even self-reports uid 65534
while the host uid stays 0). Wrappers are absolute-path; a REAL privilege drop is used (root:
`unshare --net`+`setpriv --reuid=65534`; non-root: `unshare --user --net`). (2) the design gate is a
machine-readable ```yaml `policy:` block the checker parses — prose ("isolation is unnecessary")
can no longer satisfy or invert it. (3) the forbidden-op sweep is reframed as a best-effort LINT
(alias resolution + constant folding added, catches 12 equivalents incl. `import subprocess as sp`,
`from subprocess import run`, `cmd=[…]; run(cmd)`, `mode=0o777`, `0o700|0o077`, `${VAR:-host}`) whose
limits are stated — the boundary is the runtime isolation + gVisor, not a complete static scan.
(4) the spec pins the COMPLETE four-axis reason (canonical contract ≥ the strongest test). (5) every
namespace-reading leg is `--selftest`-gated and a test proves the incapable path exits 2 (defer,
never a false pass/breach) — the round-2 "2 failed" was an ungated leg. `isinstance(True,int)` /
`uid:-1` are dissolved (uid parsed from /proc). **CI on the first round-3 push caught a
self-inflicted venue bug** (the same "test both venues" rule this increment baked): the checker
asserted `uid != parent`, which passes only as root; the non-root ubuntu runner failed
`uid-not-dropped`. Fixed — the invariant is `uid != 0` (never root, never `!= parent`; a non-root
runner's rubric inherits its non-root uid), the uid-drop discrimination is gated to a root venue,
and `test_positive_conformance_non_root_venue` (setpriv-simulated) exercises the CI path
in-sandbox. Both venues green. 33 S0-11 tests, 144 full suite, ×2 deterministic;
3-leg result.json; ledger integrity green; `execution_proof` 1 of 7. Registry: AF-AP-24/25/26
extended, AF-AP-28 (trust the subject's self-report), AF-AP-29 (contract weaker than the test),
AF-AP-30 (specimen-only remediation) added; bakes to `anti-hollow-green` (equivalence-class mutants)
and `build-loop` (both-venue leg testing). S0-09/S0-10 regex checkers remain OPEN siblings of
AF-AP-25 (owner "3 checkers") — flagged, not yet swept.
